電機機械  
上銀(2049.TT) 
AI 與半導體需求強勁，精密線性傳動元件交期持續拉長

AI 與半導體需求強勁，帶動工具機新一波的景氣循環，上銀生產
的精密線性傳動元件大量用於 AI 與半導體設備 ， 在半導體產業CapEx
確立的情況下，預期這波拉貨潮將延續至明年。在上一波 2021-22 年
的上升循環中，股價最高來到當年度 EPS 的 45 倍，考量到目前僅出
現第一波漲價，研究部以 2027 年 EPS 13.16 元給予 35x 本益比，目
標價為 460 元，給予買進的評等。

1. 半導體設備需求強勁，帶動工具機新一波的景氣循環 
上銀生產的線性滑軌與滾珠螺桿大量應用於 AI 與半導體設備當中，這
波景氣上升循環由從 4Q25 開始，拉貨動能逐季提升。從交期來看，線
性滑軌的交期從去年年底 3 個月，拉長到現在的 4~5 個月；滾珠螺桿
交期從去年年底 4 個月，拉長到現在 5~6 個月。從營收來看，扣除 2
月份有過年因素外 (去年過年在 1 月)，上銀的月營收 YoY 從 3 月的
17.4%一路提升到 8 月的 42.3%，此期間沒有縮小過，顯示營收不但在
成長，成長的動能還在加速。公司亦提到從 6 月開始 BB raio 大於 1，
需求非常強勁，已開始規劃明年過年期間要如何加班生產。研究部看好
這波由 AI 與半導體帶動的工具機需求延續至明年，預估上銀 2026/27
年營收為 300.1/378.2 億元，+23.7%/+26.0% YoY。

2. 市場供不應求，交期持續拉長看好後續漲價機會 
市場供不應求 ， 公司於今年3 月宣布調漲 7~15%， 已於6 月開始反應 。
2Q26 毛利率 34.2%，季增 2.2 個百分點，為稼動率與價格同步提升所
致，預估 3Q26 漲價效應完全反應後毛利率將進一步提升至 35.2%，研
究部預估上銀 2026/27 年的毛利率為 34.3%/35.6%， 分別年增5.5/1.3
個百分點，EPS 為 9.56/13.16 元，+121.7%/+37.6% YoY。 
這波由半導體產業 CapEx 所帶動的景氣上升，研究部看好在交期不斷拉長
的情況下，後續還有漲價、上修毛利率的機會。(上一波 2021-2022 年上升
循環中毛利率最高來到 37-38%)

一、 一、公司介紹 
   上銀科技成立於 1989 年 ， 專注於線性滑軌 、 滾珠螺桿與單軸機器人等精密線性
傳動元件的製造 ， 為該領域的全球領導廠商之一 。 除了持續研發線性傳動產品以外 ，
也自行開發出多種半導體自動化設備 ，產品廣泛應用於汽車 、 半導體 、 醫療 、 國防 、
航太及食品等產業。上銀憑藉自有品牌 「HIWIN」 的深厚研發實力與品質行銷全世
界，目前為全球第二大生產線性滑軌與滾珠螺桿的廠商，第一大為日商 THK 
(6481.T)。1H26 產品營收占比﹔線性滑軌 60%、 滾珠螺桿20%、 工業機器人12%、
其他 8%。2025 年地區營收占比﹔台灣 44%、中國 21%、德國 16%、美國 5%、
日本 4%、其他 10%。(地區營收 2025 年已是最新公布)

二、 產業分析 
上銀主要產品為線性滑軌與滾珠螺桿，產品功能如下﹔ 
1. 線性滑軌﹔一種線性傳動元件，由滑塊與滑軌組成，透過內部的滾珠或滾  
柱循環系統來降低摩擦，讓平台保持平行穩定的直線運動。  
2. 滾珠螺桿﹔一種線性傳動元件，由螺桿、螺帽與鋼珠組成，利用鋼珠在螺桿與
螺帽間循環滾動，將旋轉運動轉換為直線運動。

圖四、滾珠螺桿

商業模式 
線性滑軌與滾珠螺桿為工具機零組件 ， 工具機產業商業模式分為以下三種 (以 AOI
檢測設備為例)﹔ 
1. 工具機零組件廠生產線性滑軌 ➨ 工具機 OEM 廠將各零組件組裝成五軸加工
機 ➨ 設備零組件廠用五軸加工機生產高精度定位平台  ➨ 設備商用高精度定位
平台與其他零組件組裝成 AOI 檢測設備。  
2. 工具機零組件廠生產線性滑軌 ➨ 工具機 OEM 廠將各零組件組裝成五軸加工
機 ➨ 設備商用五軸加工機自行生產所需的高精度定位平台或其他零組件。 
3. 工具機零組件廠生產的線性滑軌同時是工具機零組件與設備零組件  ➨ 設備
商直接購買線性滑軌來組裝。

圖五、商業模式

資料來源：統一投顧整理

### Page 5

請參見末頁重要披露及免責聲明 5

產業趨勢 
JMTBA (日本工具機協會) 每月會將日本所有工具機 OEM 廠的訂單加總 ， 公布 「日
本工具機訂單金額」 ，其產品包含多軸加工機、CNC 車床、滾齒機……等等，由於
日本擁有全球規模前幾大的工具機 OEM 廠，如 DMG、Yamazaki、Okuma，且多
年來產品遍布各個產業所累積的經驗也使得這些公司不論在自研控制器的演算
法，還是機器長時間運轉下維持的精度與溫度都與其他國家有技術上的差距。  
因此，在各行各業景氣轉好，需要大量的工具機時，JMTBA 公布的訂單金額被視
為是工具機產業最重要的觀察指標，工具機 OEM 廠訂單增加 ➨ 增加向工具機
零組件廠的拉貨。 
觀察過去十年日本工具機訂單的走勢，可發現景氣大約呈現兩年上升、兩年下降
的循環。上一次的上升週期為 2021-22 年疫情加上塞港，全球各產業瘋狂拉貨，
使得工具機訂單 YoY 從 2020/11 的-6%，最高拉升到 2021/5 的 142%，一直到
2022/10 訂單 YoY 才轉負 。 塞港造成的恐慌式補庫存為工具機產業帶來20 年來不
曾有過的成長率 ， 也使得隨後各產業去化庫存時 ， 工具機這次的景氣休息得較久 ，
直到 2H25 才再次迎來上升週期。

圖六、日本工具機訂單及 YoY

資料來源：JMTBA

圖七、上銀過去十年股價走勢圖

資料來源：Wind 
（註：與日本工具機訂單 YoY 高度聯動）

而在此波的上升週期，生產線性滑軌與滾珠螺桿等工具機零組件全球市占前二的
廠商，THK、上銀一致提到這一次的需求來自 AI 與半導體，且需求非常強勁。 
細看 AI 與半導體產業，其中涵蓋了相當多工具機的蹤跡，舉凡伺服器散熱所需的
水冷板、快接頭及熱交換器，到伺服器機殼、Compute/GPU Tray 與各種結構件，
都需要以工具機進行切削或沖壓。半導體產業中，在製程升級與產能擴充的過程
需要大量的半導體設備 ，不論在前 、 中 、 後段的設備都須使用線性傳動元件 ， 如蝕
刻機、AOI 檢測設備、各類型 Bonder 等等。 
產品規格方面，AI 與半導體所需的工具機精度更高，使得 THK、上銀這種技術領
先的廠商進一步受惠 。 一般工業用工具機僅需使用H/N 級的線性滑軌與 C5/C7 級
的滾珠螺桿，生產水冷板與快接頭時需使用 SP/P 級的線性滑軌與 C3 級的滾珠螺
桿；在Die Bonder (晶粒鍵合機)與 Flip Chip Bonder (倒裝鍵合機)上則需使用 UP/SP
級的線性滑軌與 C0/C1 級的滾珠螺桿，生產高精度的線性滑軌與滾珠螺桿毛利率
顯著高於精度較低的。

表二、線性滑軌規格

SP ≈ 7 μm

除了做元件以外，上銀也與大銀微系統合作製造半導體整機設備，由上銀做線性
滑軌與滾珠螺桿 ，大銀微提供各類馬達 、 定位平台 、 驅動器與運動控制器等等 ，產
品包含 EFEM ( 晶圓移載系統 )、Load Port ( 晶圓裝卸機)、Wafer Robot ( 晶圓機器
人) 、Wafer Aligner (晶圓尋邊器)，目前認列於機器人營收當中，產能滿載，直接
出貨給半導體製程廠商。

小結 
歷經 2022 年後的全球各產業庫存去化，工具機景氣委靡了接近三年的期間，如
今挾帶著 AI 伺服器與半導體設備需求強勁成長，再次迎來新一波景氣上升循環。
從水冷板、快接頭到各類半導體設備，皆帶動工具機需求快速升溫， 相關廠商也
同步指出需求強勁、訂單持續增加，交期明顯拉長。 
更重要的是，這波需求主要來自半導體設備等高精度應用領域，所需線性滑軌與
滾珠螺桿規格高於一般工業產品，上銀除受惠於工具機景氣重新向上外，亦可進
一步受惠於高規格精密線性傳動元件需求成長。

三、 三、營運狀況 
1. 1. 半導體設備需求強勁，帶動工具機新一波的景氣循環 
上銀生產的線性滑軌與滾珠螺桿大量應用於半導體設備當中，這波景氣上升循
環由從 4Q25 開始，拉貨動能逐季提升。從交期來看，線性滑軌的交期從去年
年底 3 個月 ，到五月法說會提到 3.5 個月 ， 再到八月份交期已經拉長到 5 個月 ；
滾珠螺桿交期從去年年底 4 個月，到五月法說會的 4.5 個月，再到現在的 6 個
月。從營收來看，扣除 2 月份有過年因素外 (去年過年在 1 月)，上銀的月營收
YoY 從 3 月的 17.4%一路提升到 8 月的 42.3%，此期間沒有縮小過，顯示營收
不但在成長，成長的動能還在加速。公司亦提到從6 月開始 BB raio 大於 1，需
求非常強勁，已開始規劃明 年過年期間要如何加班生產。研究部看好這波半導
體帶動的工具機需求延續至明年 ，預估上銀 2026/27 年營收為 300.1/378.2 億
元，+23.7%/+26.0% YoY。

圖十二、上銀各產品交期

資料來源：統一投顧整理

2. 2. 市場供不應求，交期持續拉長看好後續漲價機會 
市場供不應求，公司於今年 3 月宣布調漲 7~15%，已於 6 月開始反應。2Q26 毛
利率 34.2%，季增 2.2 個百分點，為稼動率與價格同步提升所致，預估 3Q26 漲價
效應完全反應後毛利率將進一步提升至 35.2%，研究部預估上銀 2026/27 年的毛
利率為 34.3%/35.6%，分別年增 5.5/1.3 個百分點 ，EPS 為 9.56/13.16 元，
+121.7%/+37.6% YoY。這波由半導體產業 CapEx 所帶動的景氣上升，研究部看
好在交期不斷拉長的情況下，後續還有漲價、上修毛利率的機會。 (上一波 2021-
2022 年上升循環中毛利率由 31%迅速拉升至 37-38%)

3. 預期景氣將延續至明年，目前評價偏低 
AI 與半導體需求強勁，帶動工具機新一波的景氣循環，上銀生產的精密線性傳動
元件大量用於 AI 與半導體設備，在半導體產業 CapEx 確立的情況下，預期這波
拉貨潮將延續至明年。在上一波 2021-22 年的上升循環中，股價最高來到當年度
EPS 的 45 倍，考量到目前僅出現第一波漲價，研究部以 2027 年 EPS 13.16 元給
予 35x 本益比，目標價為 460 元，給予買進的評等。

### Page 1

•MediaTek becomes a much more credible AI ASIC contender . MediaTek 
already expects its first major AI accelerator ASIC to enter production in 
4Q26, has guided to >$2bn of data-center revenue in 2026, estimates a 
roughly US$80bn 2027 addressable/serviceable market, and has raised its 
target share to 15–20%.   The NVIDIA relationship therefore potentially 
expands MediaTek’s opportunity beyond its existing hyperscaler programs: 
NVLink Fusion can become a customer-acquisition platform for future 
second customer's ASIC wins as NVLink Fusion changes the competitive 
proposition: instead of MediaTek selling customers only an independently 
designed ASIC, it can potentially offer a custom XPU already engineered to 
coexist with NVIDIA GPUs, CPUs, networking and rack-scale NVLink 
infrastructure. According to NVIDIA, the cooperation will be deepening 
including : NVIDIA NVLink Fusion chiplet, connecting XPUs to the NVIDIA 
NVLink scale-up fabric using NVIDIA photonics or electrical 
interconnects;  NVIDIA NVLink-C2C, providing high-bandwidth, energy-
efficient connectivity between XPUs, NVIDIA Rosa CPUs and other 
compatible processors. NVIDIA NVHBM, integrating customized memory 
capabilities to increase bandwidth and energy efficiency while dedicating 
more silicon area to compute.
•MediaTek is evolving from a smartphone SoC company into an AI 
compute-platform . MediaTek and NVIDIA announced the GB10 platform 
last year, where MediaTek contributed Arm CPU/SoC and power-efficiency 
expertise to the Grace Blackwell-based DGX Spark platform.   That 
relationship has already expanded into RTX Spark for Windows AI PCs, and 
today’s announcement explicitly says the two companies intend to

collaborate across multiple generations of both RTX Spark and DGX Spark products.   MediaTek therefore gains three
separate AI growth vectors: (1) hyperscaler custom AI ASICs, (2) NVIDIA-based AI PCs/local AI systems, and (3)
automotive/physical AI. Strategically, this is important because MediaTek can reuse the same core capabilities—
leading-edge CPU/SoC design, SerDes/interconnect, advanced packaging, connectivity and power optimization—

across all three markets. The result should be a progressively higher mix of large, advanced-node chips with much
greater dollar content than traditional smartphone SoCs, reducing MediaTek’s dependence on handset cycles.
•We view today’s announcement as structurally positive for MediaTek. The US$3.5bn investment validates the
partnership financially, it potentially moves MediaTek from being a supplier that happens to have a few large AI ASIC
wins toward becoming one of the principal custom-silicon design platforms inside the broader NVIDIA AI-factory

ecosystem. That could materially increase both the number and longevity of MediaTek’s AI ASIC opportunities in the
longer term.

### Page 3

Mediatek (2454.TW)
31 August 2026 Citi Research
3
Mediatek
Valuation
Our NT$6,800 target price (rounded) for MediaTek is based on 35x P/E to 2027/2028 EPS, which is 1.5-std above its five-year
average forward P/E given the promising outlook from its AI ASIC business starting from 2H26. We believe our target PE is also
justified by Mediatek's global and regional peers' trading at an average of 36x PER. AI ASIC development with CSPs should
provide more upside from 2027 onwards. We also see Mediatek well prepared for 6G and 6G+NTN Satellite communication.

Despite lukewarm smartphone overall deamand, we expect Mediatek to benefit from 5G migration in emerging markets and its
Dimensity series should also gain shares in flagship models. Non-smartphone business is also seeing stabilizing TV and IoT
demand with Mediatek outgrowing the overall market on its better technology in WiFi 7 and connectivity.
Risks
Key downside risks that could prevent the shares from reaching our target price include: 1) weaker-than-expected growth from

AI ASIC business; 2) more severe competition in the smartphone segment; 3) slower overall demand related to macroeconomic
risks, especially for smartphone demand; and 4) further price hikes from foundries.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

Update
Accton Technology Corporation  | Asia Pacific
August Sales +4% MoM/+59%
YoY
In this report, we focus on Accton's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for share price movement.
Details:
• August sales were NT$40,873mn (+4% MoM/+59% YoY).
• QTD reached 66% of our 3Q26 estimate, at NT$120,883mn (+27% QoQ/
+66% YoY), and 70% of consensus, at NT$114,321mn (+20% QoQ/+57%
YoY).
Our view:
• We see 3Q26 revenue tracking in line with our estimates

• Our checks indicate that Accton has been negotiating with customers to let
them consign  those expensive components, which could weigh on its
revenue growth but should be positive to margins.
• Looking ahead, we expect the momentum to remain elevated into 2H26, as it
should benefit from broader-based migration to 800G network switches
from both existing and new customers, and the ramp of the new AI
accelerator model from its major customer should be in full swing.

• Stay OW with a PT of NT$3,500, based on 30x 2027 P/E.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Accton Technology Corporation (2345.TW)
Base case, P/E, which we believe better captures the company's earnings growth momentum.
We assign a target P/E of 30x to our 2027 EPS estimate, which we view as justified by an
operating profit CAGR of 48% for 2025-28, based on our estimates. This implies a PEG of
around 0.6, vs. its average of 0.7 over 2019-25, when the stock was trading at a P/E of 22x for

an operating profit CAGR of 32%.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger data center capex
n Faster network switch spec migration
n InfiniBand losing share in AI networks
n Stronger custom AI accelerator growth
n Stronger ODM direct business model adoption
Risks to Downside
n Weaker data center capex
n Slower network switch spec migration
n InfiniBand taking more share in AI networks
n Softer custom AI accelerator growth
n Softer ODM direct business model adoption

Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

Summary: Driven by strong AI/HPC demand, all three companies set new revenue 
milestones: Winway (TWD 1.71bn, +6.6% MoM, +233.4% YoY), MPI (TWD 2.05bn, 
+13.4% MoM, +69.9% YoY), and CHPT (TWD 638m, +3.9% MoM, +54.3% YoY). With 
Winway’s capacity expansions tracking ahead of schedule, MPI set to begin shipping CPO 
insertion 3 products in 4Q26 alongside ASP hikes in late-3Q26, and CHPT nearing a 
qualification for its turnkey CPU ASIC project, the sector offers resilient sequential growth 
visibility through 2H26 and potential earnings upside heading into 2027.

What’s new: Following the conclusion of SEMICON Taiwan, we have observed a marked 
uptick in investor enquiries regarding the semiconductor test interface supply chain. As 
AI/HPC architectural transitions, and the increasing complexity of 2.5D/3D packaging drive 
structural content gains, investors have focused increasingly on capacity visibility and 
technological roadmaps across the socket and probe card segments. In the note, we recap 
the latest August monthly revenue figures and synthesize key market dynamics across our 
coverage universe.

WinWay Technology (6515 TT, TWD7,280, Buy [1]) 
• Aug revenue result: Monthly revenue reached TWD 1.71bn ( +6.6% MoM, +233.4% 
YoY), bringing 1-8M26 cumulative revenue to TWD 9.81bn (+97.9% YoY). Jul-Aug sales 
reached 72% of our 3Q26E revenue, tracking ahead of our expectations.

• Product mix & catalysts: Growth remains anchored by aggressive tape-outs from 
North American GPU leaders and cloud hyperscalers. Shipments of proprietary coaxial 
test sockets and advanced wafer-level vertical probe cards expanded sharply. While the 
market may expect meaningful revenue contributions from CPO sockets or probe cards, 
we believe it will account for mid-single digit in 2027E and high-single digit in 2028E.

• Outlook: The company maintained a constructive stance on order visibility and 
persistent demand-supply tightness. Management reaffirmed that 2Q26 marked the 
near-term cyclical trough, guiding for sequential revenue expansion on a QoQ basis 
moving forward. Our supply chain checks indicate that capacity expansion execution is 
tracking ahead of schedule compared with last month. With Winway poised to bring 
additional capacity online in 2027, we suggest investors monitor the company’s 2H26 
earnings call (mid-Nov) for further clarity on its 2027 capex and capacity roadmap.

• Valuation: We have a Buy (1) rating with a 12-month TP of TWD10,000, based on a 
target PER of 70x applied to our 4-quarter-forward EPS forecast. Based on our 
2027/28E EPS, the stock is currently trading at PERs of 36.9x/19.5x, vs. its previous 
highs of 80-90x. Downside risks: heavy reliance on major US AI chipmakers and; 2) 
intensifying competition that could erode design wins.

MPI Corporation (6223 TT, TWD5,615, Buy[1]) 
• Aug revenue result: Monthly revenue surpassed the TWD 2bn milestone for the first 
time, reaching TWD 2.05bn (+13.4% MoM, +69.9% YoY), bringing 1-8M26 cumulative 
revenue above TWD 13.0bn. Jul-Aug sales reached 63% of our 3Q26E revenue 
estimate, tracking slightly below our expectations.

• Product mix & catalysts: Vertical Probe Cards (VPC) and MEMS probe cards are 
operating at near-full capacity to support volume ramps in HPC, AI accelerators, and 
high-speed networking ICs. MPI’s AST (Advanced Semiconductor Test) and Thermal 
test chambers also maintained solid delivery momentum to European and US 
automotive and R&D laboratories. CPO insertion 3 engineering test equipment is 
scheduled for delivery in 4Q26, setting the stage for mass production orders in 1H27.

• Outlook: Although management’s muted 3Q26 guidance initially fuelled investor 
scepticism, and the cumulative 2M26 run-rate is tracking slightly behind our quarterly 
forecast, we view the market’s pessimism as overly conservative. Strong late-quarter 
shipment seasonality, coupled with concentrated September revenue recognition for 
high-end VPC/MEMS probe cards under the newly effective pricing schedule should 
help bridge the gap and keep 3Q26 on track to meet or exceed our top-line 
expectations. Looking into 4Q26 and early 2027, the commercial rollout of CPO 
insertion 3 equipment and continued certification progress for insertion 2 provide a multi-
year growth runaway and durable operating leverage.

• Valuation: We have a Buy (1) rating with a 12-month TP of TWD8,415, based on a 
target PER of 65x applied to our 4-quarter-forward EPS forecast. Based on our 
2027/28E EPS, the stock is currently trading at PERs of 42.9x/26.0x, vs. its peak of 80x. 
Downside risks: 1) Competitive and pricing pressure; and 2) delays due to AI/HPC 
slowdown.

Chunghwa Precision Test Tech (CHPT, 6510 TT, TWD3,610, Buy [1]) 
• Aug revenue result: August revenue totalled TWD 638m (+3.9% MoM, +54.3% YoY), 
bringing 1-8M26 cumulative revenue to TWD 4.25bn (+33.14% YoY). Jul-Aug sales 
reached 66% of our 3Q26E revenue, tracking in line with our expectations. In response 
to Taiwan Stock Exchange (TWSE) inquiries following recent share price volatility, CHPT 
disclosed its August financial results. Single-month EPS came in at TWD 5.29 (net 
income of TWD 174m, +95% YoY), implying a quarterly EPS run-rate of TWD 15.87 (vs 
Daiwa estimate of TWD 16.08 and market consensus of TWD 17.52)

• Product mix & catalysts: Top-line momentum was broad-based across both the AI 
GPU and ASIC segments. Management highlighted ongoing footprint expansion and 
continued efforts to identify additional sites to scale manufacturing capacity. Moving 
forward, steady deliveries of AI GPU test boards, alongside the forthcoming volume 
ramp of custom AI ASICs, should underpin sequential revenue growth.

• Outlook: Our supply chain checks indicate CHPT is competing for three AI/HPC probe 
card turnkey programs, comprising a CPU ASIC, an AI ASIC program for a US 
automotive client, and another AI ASIC program for Taiwanese clients. The automaker 
qualification process is progressing smoothly, and CHPT plans to ship engineering 
probe cards for validation in November, with validation results expected by end-2026. 
Given the superior content value of probe card solutions versus board-only products, a 
successful qualification would provide incremental top-line upside into late-2026, while 
serving as a significant catalyst for 2027 revenue upgrades and valuation expansion.

• Valuation: We have a Buy (1) rating with a 12-month TP of TWD4,000, based on a 
target PER of 45x applied to our 4-quarter-forward EPS forecast. Based on our 
2027/28E EPS, the stock is currently trading at PERs of 30.2x/24.0x, vs. its peak of 68x. 
Downside risks: 1) project delays and utilisation risk; and 2) loss of market share.

400G ZR/ZR+ 目前維持穩定出貨，雷射晶片後段產能亦配合客戶
需求持續擴增；隨 800G ZR/ZR+ 預計於 2026H2 開始放量，公司
後續成長動能可望進一步提升。 產能方面， 華星光無塵室面積預計
於 2026 年成長 24%，2027 年再增加 67%，新增產能將同步支應
雷射產品及光收發模組代工需求。綜合以上，研究部預估華星光
2026 年 EPS 7.95 元，2027 年 EPS 16.99 元，目前 PE 為 36x，
參考國內光通訊類股 PE 區間為 20-50x，考量下一世代 800G 
ZR/ZR+產品將開始加入貢獻，另外雷射後段產品也將加入 EML，
營運成長動能顯著，因此給予 40xPER，目標價 680 元，建議區
間操作。  
⚫ 公司基本資料 ⚫ 公司基本資料 
華星光(4979)成立於 2001 年，為國內光通訊主動元件生產廠，產品
包含雷射晶粒、次模組、光收發模組代工。2025 年光通訊主動元件
及模組佔 86%、晶粒 11%、其他 3%，銷售地區則以北美為主，佔
87%、台灣 11%、其他 2%。生產基地在中壢合江廠與吉林廠，預
計新增竹東和八德廠。

⚫ 近期報酬率(%) ⚫ 營運概況 
伴隨雷射客戶需求出貨，26H1 晶粒營收 Y+69% 
華星光 26Q2 營收 12.9 億元 ，Q+8.0%，Y+18.5%， 毛利率22.44%，
稅後淨利 1.8 億元 ，Q-19.8%，Y+94.7%，EPS 1.29 元。26H1 400G 
ZR/ZR+需求穩定出貨，營收成長動能主要來自於晶粒產品，隨著雷
射客戶需求的成長，華星光 26H1 晶粒營收 Y+69%。然而由於財稅
認列時點影響毛利率，Q-2.98ppts，預計 Q3 會恢復提列。

800G ZR/ZR+放量貢獻，竹東廠加入投產  
華星光 7 月營收 5.1 億元 ，M+11.8%，Y+31.0%， 其中 ，400G ZR/ZR+
出貨維持穩定，800G ZR/ZR+則逐步進入放量成長階段，帶動光收
發模組代工業務成為新的營運成長動能。另一方面，因應雷射客戶
持續擴充產能，公司同步加大無塵室投資，竹東廠預計於 26Q3 加
入營運，進一步擴充整體生產量能，支撐後續雷射相關產品需求成
長 。 綜合以上 ， 研究部預估華星光26Q3 營收 16.7 億元 ，Q+29.8%，
Y+55.5% ，毛利率 27.83% ，稅後淨利 3.4 億元， Q+82.7% ，
Y+54.6%，EPS 2.36 元。

2026 年底晶粒產能翻倍，2027 年 DCI 產品貢獻翻倍 
根據 Yole 統計 ，2025 年全球 400G ZR/ZR+需求量約 850 萬支 ，2026
年預估增至 1,000 萬支 ，2027 年則維持約 975 萬支水準 ； 另一方面 ，
800G ZR/ZR+ 開始進入快速成長期，2026 年出貨量預估約 700 萬
支，2027 年將進一步增至 1,000 萬支，並有望於 2028 年後逐步成
為 DCI 市場主流規格，帶動高速光模組需求持續升級。雷射產品亦
同步朝高功率方向發展，目前主流功率約 70/100mW，未來將逐步
提升至 400mW 以上，高功率產品所需晶片面積亦將顯著增加，進
一步推升晶圓與後段封裝產能需求。因應客戶需求成長，公司持續
擴充產能 ，2025 年底整體無塵室樓地板面積約 6,550 平方米 ，2026
日 週 月

精測 (6510 TT)  Buy

精測受惠 HPC 測試需求， 2H26 營運優於 1H26，給 Buy 的
投資評等。  
投資建議 ：精測受惠於 HPC 測試需求， 3Q26 營運可望持續季
增、 2027 年營運也將優於 2026 年，給 Buy 的投資評等。  
輝達入股聯發科，有助精測短期評價 ：聯發科 (2454 TT) 持股翔
發投資 100% ，翔發投資持股精測約 0.5% 。輝達 (NVDA US) 認
購聯發科的 ECB 約 35 億美元，有助聯發科 \精測的短期評價與
中長期營運。  
精測有 TPU 與 AI5 商機 ：精測表示，美系客戶的 TPU 供應商
維持既有架構，預期在  TPU8 專案中將扮演一定角色，不過目
前尚未正式發包，相關專案預計於 2H26 啟動。此外，針對另一
客戶的  AI5 專案，依客戶規劃時程，預計 06/2026 進行工程卡
交付，並預計於 2026 年底至 2027 年初進入量產。  
精測新產能持續開出 ：2026 年營運逐季成長趨勢明確， 3Q26 營
收有機會雙位數季增， 08/2026 、 03/2027 會有比較大的產能開
出。目前交期約 5~8 周，新產能客戶驗證時間約需一季， 2027
年營運健康成長。  
精測 3Q26 毛利率可能季減 ：3Q26 毛利率區間為 50~55% ，因新
產品一開始會有學習曲線需爬升，後段測試 \老化測試的毛利率
也較低。估 2H26 每季費用約 3.8~4.2 億元，全年費用率約
20~25% 。

個股報告

2 群益投顧 
  ESG ：  
在環境保護方面，中華精測於 2022 年開始自主性導入溫室氣體盤查作業，
除了計算碳使用總量外，也了解如何節能及尋找替代能源，並推動各項環保
節能減碳措施、減少溫室氣體排放量等，以期減緩與調適氣候變遷所帶來之
營運衝擊。  
在社會責任方面，中華精測遵守相關法規及國際人權公約，持續營造友善、
平等、自由的工作環境，對於提升員工福祉不遺餘力，且不定期舉辦家庭活
動及獎勵同仁結婚、生育、旅遊津貼等員工福利。  
在公司治理營運績效方面，中華精測連續兩年於公司治理評鑑排名前 5%，
並於 2022 年榮獲上櫃公司治理評鑑  Top5% 之佳績，秉持誠信正直原則，定
期宣導及教育訓練內線交易、誠信廉潔等法律知識，確保公司員工遵守誠信
經營守則之落實。  
公司簡介：  
中華精測科技股份有限公司 (6510 TT) 設立於 08/2005 ，總部及生產工廠皆位
於桃園平鎮，為中華電信所屬關係企業，前身為中華電信研究所內部之高速
PCB 團隊， 2001 年起開始嘗試研發設計測試載板 (Load board) 及探針卡載板
(Probe PCB) 。  
中華精測主要從事晶圓測試板、 IC 測試板、技術服務與其他等業務，提供
半導體產業測試所需的介面板服務，用於晶圓測試之探針卡專用印刷電路
板 (Probe PCB) ，及用於 IC 測試之載版 (Load Board) 。  
中華電信 (2412 TT) 持股中華投資公司約 89.0% ；中華投資持股精測約
33.6% ；聯發科 (2454 TT) 於 2015 年，透過旗下翔發投資公司入股中華精測，
目前持股約 0.5% 。  
精測的台灣同業包含旺矽 (6223 TT) 、穎崴 (6515 TT) 、雍智科技 (6683 TT)…
等。精測的客戶包含美系 HPC 公司、台系 IC 設計公司、美系 IC 設計公司，
台系晶圓代工廠 … 等。  
營收占比 _產品別：  
晶圓測試卡包含 PCB 與整組探針卡。

資料來源：精測法說會簡報。

3 群益投顧 
營收占比 _產品應用別：

資料來源：精測法說會簡報。  
精測 3Q26 毛利率可能季減 ：  
3Q26 毛利率區間為 50~55% ，因新產品一開始會有學習曲線需爬升，後段測
試 \老化測試的毛利率也較低。估 2H26 每季費用約 3.8~4.2 億元，全年費用
率約 20~25% 。  
精測新產能持續開出 ：  
2026 年營運逐季成長趨勢明確， 3Q26 營收有機會雙位數季增， 08/2026 、
03/2027 會有比較大的產能開出。目前交期約 5~8 周，新產能客戶驗證時間
約需一季。 2027 年營運健康成長。  
精測有發 CB，三廠廠能預估 4Q28 開出：  
精測 07/29 重訊決議發行 CB，預計募資 20 億元，主要用於新廠的工程款，
其餘用於營運、新材料、新產品的研發。三廠預計 4Q25 開始動工、 4Q28 貢
獻營收，新產能是舊產能的 100% 以上。  
其他補充：  
美系車用客戶的 AI 晶片，精測目前有在送樣測試產品，驗證進度 85% 。  
2026 年 CAPEX 從 3.5 億元提高到 5.0 億元，用在擴充產能、承租廠房； 2026
全年折舊金額約 4.1 億元。  
TPU 改用 EMIB-T 封裝，之後還是要回台灣做測試，對精測影響不大。原物
料上漲，精測會反映給客戶，但精測不會因為供需吃緊而漲價。

7 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• In something as complex as an economy, outcomes often
defy simple narratives. It is a little like those inkblot
psychology tests one sometimes sees. Is it a rabbit or is it a
lady? It depends on your perspective.
• The national accounts for Q2 2026 were a case in point.

A hawkish take would focus on GDP growth being above
RBA expectations and not below its view of trend; a dovish
take would focus on the loss of momentum more recently.
Likewise a hawk could point to the outsized July CPI print,
while the dove can remind you of the noise and uncertain
seasonality in the monthly data. Both narratives inherently
leave out contradicting signals.
• The key issue here is to make sure that you are taking signal
from all the available data, not just the things that fit the

narrative. Labour market data deserve more consideration
than some observers are giving them. Also important is
remembering that the next quarter will always be thrown
about by special factors, and what matters is where things
are in a year or so’s time.
• What is the resolution, if any? Structured analysis including
whole-economy models is one way through the difficulty,
but it relies on the models being a decent representation
of reality. Perhaps another, honouring the fact our brains

evolved to want simple stories, is to emulate Charles
Darwin and systematically note down any piece of evidence
against your current theory. It might make it harder to tell
a simple story, but it will mean you are less likely to miss
something that later turns out to be important.
In something as complex as an economy, there are always
many things going on, not all pointing in the same direction.
There will be headwinds and tailwinds. Most shocks and

trends affect some industries and parts of the economy more
than others. The overall impact is a complicated netting
exercise that is hard to be precise about in the real world,
where measurement is uncertain and things get revised.
That complexity sits uncomfortably for a species that shapes
its understanding through story. A simple cause-and-
effect narrative fits the way our brains have evolved. Messy
outcomes like this week’s national accounts (and the domestic

data flow more broadly) do not fit simple stories. It is tempting
for people to extract one nonetheless. The process resembles
one of those old psychology tricks where you can see a rabbit
or a woman, a face or a vase, depending on your perspective,
or the Rorschach inkblot where you can “see” what you want
The national accounts inkblot test
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
to see. We pattern-match, we see creatures in the clouds – and
sometimes, we can be selective with data when we should not

everyone else. That said, I cannot help noticing how easy it
is to squint at the economic inkblot and see a hawk or a dove
according to one’s prior beliefs.
Those seeing the hawk will point to (among other things) the
2.1%yr GDP growth being above the RBA’s forecast and not
below its estimate of potential output growth “speed limit”, as
the RBA is trying to engineer. They will note the tick-up in unit
labour cost growth, resilient consumption, strong data centre

investment and – beyond the national accounts – the outsized
July CPI inflation data. Putting these together, a narrative
forms that seemingly demands immediate further tightening of
monetary policy.
In doing so, though, one would need to leave a few things
out. Among these are the fact that growth stepped down
noticeably through the year, and that about half the upside
surprise in year-ended growth was a revision to an earlier
quarter, implying you got more growth for the same amount of

inflation. Also downplayed in this narrative is the weak growth
in average earnings per hour – a less-noisy input into unit
labour costs – the increase in household saving and the roll-off
in public sector investment making room for higher dwelling
and data centre investment.
And for those confidently expecting a September hike, it
requires a belief that the RBA will go back on their previous
statement that, in this initial phase, they will focus on the

quarterly inflation data rather than a new monthly series
with its short history and uncertain seasonality that is
prone to revision. (And the RBA might well go back on that
commitment, but one must explicitly believe it will.)
Forecast changes
• We are currently in the process of updating our forecasts. We
have not included any changes in this edition of the Australia &
NZ Weekly. A full update to our forecasts will be published in
our Market Outlook publication on September 11.

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
The same exercise of leaving pieces out to make a neater
story also permeates the dove case. One could highlight that
the 2.1% year-ended GDP growth rate was comprised of two
halves, with the most recent showing much weaker growth in
domestic demand. One can point to the larger-than-expected

downturn in the housing market, weak per capita growth,
moderate consumption outside a few special factors such as
EV purchases, and the likely run of monthly trimmed mean CPI
prints from here being noticeably below the July figure.
This narrative also requires leaving a few things out, though.
As well as the components of the hawk case already noted,
there is the issue that the data centre boom could well offset
the receding public sector’s release of construction resources.

Part of the difficulty is that the narratives we seek can be so
black-and-white binary. It is also embedded in the here and
now, immediate data flow. We should ask ourselves whether
we should really take so much signal from small surprises
in the latest data. The crucial question is what these noisy
outcomes mean for the underlying trends, and where growth
and inflation will be in a year or so.
We also note that much of the recent discussion has made

relatively little of the labour market. It has been easing
over much of the past year or so (contra the RBA’s initial
assessment earlier this year that it was re-tightening) and
is noticeably softer than RBA expectations. Today’s labour
accounts confirm that more people have taken on secondary
jobs to address cost-of-living pressures. Labour supply, both
in terms of the number of people in the labour force and the
number of hours they want to work, has been more resilient

than many observers expected. The various measures of
underemployment are pointing to emerging spare capacity
that might not be widely recognised.
What is the resolution, if any? Structured analysis including
whole-economy models is one way through the difficulty,
but it relies on the models being a decent representation of
reality. Perhaps another, honouring the fact our brains evolved
to want simple stories, is to emulate Charles Darwin and
systematically note down any piece of evidence against your

current theory. It might make it harder to tell a simple story,
but it will mean you are less likely to miss something that later
turns out to be important.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: balancing risks to growth and inflation
Over in the US, during his address to the Jackson Hole
Symposium last weekend, Chair Warsh emphasised the
Committee’s focus on inflation risks and willingness to tighten
further if necessary.

To make his point, Chair Warsh focused attention on the most
elevated of the US’ major inflation benchmarks, headline PCE
inflation at 3.7%yr and 4.1% on a six-month annualised basis,
as well as the breadth of price gains, which remains well
above pre-pandemic levels. Chair Warsh then made clear that
the Committee “must be confident that underlying inflation
is moving to our objective, clearly and at sufficient speed”….
“Otherwise, we have work to do.” The FOMC remaining on

hold from here requires evidence of sequential disinflation
month-on-month from next week’s August report. Without
this, the market will likely fully price a hike for the September
meeting, and a follow-up come November. Overnight Federal
Governor Waller made a similar point, noting that: if "there
is continued progress toward our 2% goal, then I am willing
to support holding the policy rate at its current level"; but "if
inflation comes in hot, I would consider a rate hike".

While qualitative in nature, the Beige Book was arguably
the most informative US release of the week. Since July,
employment growth has remained subdued, with seven districts
reporting only "slight" or "modest" growth and five no change.
Upstream price pressures persist, with "input price pressures
notably elevated in manufacturing and construction"; that said,
"heightened price sensitivity among customers" continued to
limit firms' ability to pass through these higher costs.

It is one thing for producer price inflation and capacity constraints
to bolster input prices, but to hold up the CPI/PCE, these
pressures must be passed through to the consumer basket. Along
with stability in the labour market, consumers unwillingness
to accept full passthrough is why we continue to believe the
FOMC can remain on hold through 2028 if they can weather the
immediate angst over recent inflation data. If the Committee
instead choose to hike in September and/or November, corrective

cuts may be required in late-2027 or into 2028.
A prudent approach to policy was apparent in Canada this
week, the Bank of Canada’s Governing Council voting to
remain on hold. In their words, the global economy has shown
resilience and "Canadian economic activity strengthened in
the second quarter”. But "demand for labour remains subdued
and indicators point to continued excess supply ". Also, like in
the US and Australia, "there has been little evidence of higher

energy prices spreading to other components of inflation".
As for the RBNZ and other developed-world central banks,
balancing the risks around growth as well as inflation is a
challenge for the Bank of Canada, but one worth pursuing.
Australia’s Q2 GDP came in slightly above expectations at
0.4%, 2.1%yr. Upward revisions to earlier data notwithstanding,
there has been a clear loss of momentum from H2 2025 to H1
2026, from an annualised pace of 2.8% to 1.5%.

Household consumption grew just 0.4% in Q2, and this
gain was principally due to a surge in EV sales (0.27ppts
of the 0.4% gain) in response to the Middle East conflict’s
energy price spike. Highlighting the financial resilience of
the consumer, however, a stronger-than-expected lift in real
disposable incomes allowed the savings ratio to edge higher in
the quarter despite higher interest and tax payments.
Public demand was also soft in Q2, up just 0.2% as cost-of-

living support rolled off and the infrastructure work program,
most notably in NSW and Victoria, passed its peak. Business
investment meanwhile edged lower, though solid non-residential
construction tied to data centres, renewables and related
infrastructure implies the pipeline of work remains in an uptrend.
On housing, Cotality home prices  fell 0.9% in August after
downwardly revised 1.2% and 0.9% declines in June and
July. Prices are now 3.6% below their March peak nationally,

and down 4.6%/4.7% in Sydney/ Melbourne. On a rolling
3-month basis, the volume of sales is 15% lower over the year.
Unsurprisingly, the moderation in housing credit growth is
gathering pace. That said, a substantial pipeline of approved
projects means housing construction should remain a positive
force for GDP for the foreseeable future. Towards the end of
the forecast horizon in 2028 though, risks are growing, with
dwelling approvals  declining 3.6% in July.

On external trade, Q2 was not as weak as initially feared. The
current account deficit widened by just $1.8bn to $27.2bn
and net exports added 0.1ppts to GDP. Resource exports
rebounded after disruptions from cyclones, but there was also
a significant pull-back in tourism spend by Australians overseas
due to flight cancellations related to the Middle East conflict.
Talking About Trade provides further detail on these sub-
plots and the implications. Those with a particular interest in

agriculture and related trade should also dig into our latest
Quarterly Agriculture Report .
Across the Tasman, at its September meeting, the RBNZ
lifted the OCR by 25bps  to 2.75% as expected. The RBNZ’s
revised OCR forecasts are little changed from May and signal
a pause in October before a 25bp hike to 3.00% in December.
Justifying this cautious approach, a majority of MPC members
see upside risks to inflation, but all see downside risks to
growth. Westpac continues to expect a steady but determined

tightening cycle, with the next 25bp hike not forecast until
December but a peak of 4.00% seen in Q3 2027.
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
RBNZ to pause in October, but hike in time for Christmas
As expected, the RBNZ hiked the Official Cash Rate by
25bps at this week’s meeting. The accompanying minutes
and projections signalled a further OCR rise later this year.

However, rather than committing to a series of consecutive
hikes, data permitting, it appears that the RBNZ wants to
see how the economy is shaping up in the wake of ongoing
global headwinds and the 50bps of rate hikes since July. We
continue to expect the RBNZ will pause in October before
delivering another 25bp hike in December.
Appropriately balanced
The RBNZ hiked the Official Cash Rate 25bps at its September
policy meeting, taking it to 2.75%. The decision was reached

by consensus, with no vote required. The hike was widely
expected and had been signalled by the RBNZ at the time of
the previous meeting in July.
The key question is where the OCR is headed over the coming
months, with two interest rate meetings still to come before
the end of the year. And these won’t be simple decisions for
the RBNZ.
Inflation is elevated, printing at 4.1% in the year to June. And
like the RBNZ, we expect that it will remain above the 3%

upper bound of the target range at least until the middle of
next year. Much of the current strength in inflation is due to
the spike in oil prices earlier this year. However, the underlying
inflation picture is also problematic. Stripping out volatile
items like fuel, the various measures of core inflation have
been running near the top of the RBNZ’s target band for
several years now. And with fuel and other production costs
rising, underlying inflation pressures a likely to continue

running hot for a while.
In fact, the RBNZ has revised its forecast for domestic (non-
tradable) inflation higher due to concerns that increases in fuel
and other costs will add to local price pressures. That also
means that the RBNZ now expects inflation will take slightly
longer to return to 2%. Even with this upgraded inflation
outlook, the majority of MPC members (including Governor
Breman) still see upside risk to the RBNZ’s inflation forecast.

However, it’s worth noting that much of that strength in
inflation is due to factors that the RBNZ can’t easily offset
with interest rate hikes. To illustrate this, the RBNZ’s policy
statement included a special topic focused on the conduct of
monetary policy in the face of supply shocks, like oil price spikes.
Returning inflation back to 2% quickly in such circumstances can
require very large changes in the OCR and generate significant
swings in output and employment. That sort of volatility could

be damaging for the economy and labour market, especially
as the impact of rate hikes is likely to fall on sectors of the
economy that are not the source of inflation.
Instead, the RBNZ has highlighted that a more gradual
approach to returning inflation to target is the more
appropriate course. Consistent with that, RBNZ Chief
Economist Conway noted in an interview after the MPS that
“if we can get inflation down while supporting growth that's
better than getting inflation down by derailing recovery.”

Adding to those concerns, all members of the MPC agreed that
there were significant downside risks for activity, along with
related risks for the labour market. That’s despite a downward
revision to the RBNZ’s forecast for employment growth and an
upward revision to the forecast for the unemployment rate.
A particular area of uncertainty is the outlook for household
spending, which is a key driver of domestic economic activity.
Spending growth has been soft for some time, weighed down

by weakness in the jobs market and increases in living costs.
And there are big uncertainties about how strong spending
will be in the face of ongoing softness in the housing market
– housing assets account for around half of New Zealand
households’ wealth and average prices across the country have
shown no growth for three years now. Like ourselves, the RBNZ
expects house price growth will remain subdued for some time.
Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist

### Page 6

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
October pause, December hike
Balancing out the above concerns, the MPC judged that “the
OCR may need to increase further.”  However, they went on
to note that “the future OCR path is not pre-determined”  and
highlighted that future decisions will be dependent on the

strength of data. Media comments from the RBNZ highlight
that members of the MPC want to see how the economy is
tracking following the 50bps of hikes since July.
We continue to expect that the RBNZ will pause in October
and deliver a hike at its December meeting. The October
policy meeting is also very close to the General Election on 7
November, though the RBNZ has noted that this is not a factor
that influences their decisions.
The RBNZ now forecasts an average OCR in the December

quarter of 2.81% (down just slightly from 2.84% in the May
MPS). That forecast is exactly consistent with an unchanged
OCR at the 28 October meeting followed by a 25bps rate hike
at the 9 December meeting (in contrast, a 25bp hike at the
October meeting, followed by a pause in December, would have
yielded a forecast average of 2.93%). Assistant Governor Silk
commented in a media interview that “Whether it’s October
or whether it’s December is still open, but the way the track is

situated it’s more likely the latter rather than the former.”
The Record of Meeting noted that “Future policy will depend
on the Committee’s judgement of the balance of risks to
medium-term inflation. This approach allows the Committee to
observe and assess the effects of reduced monetary stimulus.”
We think that this language also suggests an expectation that
there will be a pause before the next hike is delivered.
The MPC is likely to continue debating between further

hikes and holding rates unchanged for the foreseeable
future. A hike in October remains a possibility should data
suggest a combination of persistent inflation pressures and
a strengthening economy. Data on Q2 GDP (17 September)
and the QSBO Business Confidence survey (6 October)
will be important in informing the MPC’s judgement on the
progression of the economy. The Q3 CPI later in October will
be key information on the strength of persistent inflation

pressures.
Longer term outlook
The RBNZ’s interest rate projections for 2027 and 2028 are
essentially unchanged from May. The OCR is projected to rise
gradually over the coming years to a peak of 3.28% at the end
of 2029.
For now, the MPC’s strategy remains one of removing stimulus
as opposed to moving to a restrictive stance. We think
discussions about moving the OCR beyond neutral levels will
come in 2027 and will be informed by trends in core inflation

and the strength of the economy between now and then.
We continue to expect further increases in the OCR to 4%
by September 2027 (dependent on the strength of data, as
always).

AUS: Sep Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment (index)
Sep 8, Last: 88.9
Consumer sentiment rose 6% to 88.9 in Aug. While pessimism
eased in the month, consumers remain deeply pessimistic
overall, the mood weighed down by continued uncertainty
about the path for inflation and interest rates and disruptions

emanating from conflict in the Middle East.
Developments over the last month have tilted to the downside
with: continued conflict between the US and Iran; local pump
prices pushing back above $2/litre; a stronger than expected
July CPI result raising fears of further interest rate increases; and
a broadening and intensifying correction in housing markets.
US: Aug CPI (%mth)
Sep 11, Last: 0.1
Mkt f/c: 0.4, Westpac f/c: 0.3
Chair Warsh’s address to the Jackson Hole symposium focused

attention on the divergence between annual headline inflation
and the breadth of price pressures. His point was clear. Unless
disinflation is seen consistently month-on-month, the FOMC
will have to consider tightening policy further. Federal Reserve
Governor Waller has since provided a very similar take.
In August, energy prices will bias the headline measure up
again (0.3%). However, core inflation should remain moderate
(0.2%); if realised, this will see the annual rate tick down to

2.4%yr, which is also likely to be the 6-month annualised pace.
The detail will also be in focus. To calm the FOMC’s nerves,
a continuation of negligible core goods inflation and modest
gains for core services ex-shelter is necessary.

2 of 8 August 31, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Moderate Growth, Broadening Opportunities
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
Although the Citi Economic Surprise Index has been easing over the past month as a
rising number of data releases have fallen short of elevated expectations (Exhibit 1), the
data remains broadly positive and consistent with sustained moderate growth. Indeed,
with alternating waves of headwinds and tailwinds, real consumer spending and gross

domestic product (GDP) continue to expand near their longer-run average rates even
with substantial wealth effects and strong growth in government transfer payments.
Employment and hours worked have barely increased over the past year, leaving
productivity to account for nearly all the economic growth. Combined with moderating
wage growth, this has contained unit labor costs—a positive for inflation dynamics.
Despite stubborn readings above the Federal Reserve's (Fed) 2% target inflation through

July, consumer price index data were relatively favorable, and although the latest personal
consumption expenditures (PCE) report was less reassuring, much of the upside pressure
remained concentrated in healthcare, tariff- and energy-sensitive categories, as well as
in volatile financial services that tend to follow equity-market gains. The latter inflation
category is slated to be revised lower when new index methodologies are announced
next month. Meanwhile, a broad range of other goods and services showed signs of

cooling inflation on a three-month annualized basis, as shown in Exhibit 2.
Exhibit 1: Economic Data Surprises Have Been More Mixed Over The Past Month…

Source: Bloomberg. Data as of August 26, 2026. It is not possible to invest directly in an index. Please refer to index
definitions at the end of this report. Past performance is no guarantee of future results.

Basically, tariffs, elevated oil prices, wealth effects and AI-related electronics price
inflation continue to put upward pressure on inflation and interest rates. Although higher
gasoline prices have largely offset the One Big Beautiful Bill Act’s (OBBBA) direct benefit
to consumers, the shift from a negative fiscal impulse in 2025 to a positive impulse this
year has supported aggregate demand, contributing to the stalled progress on headline
inflation. Government transfers increased rapidly in July to a new high as a share of

personal income, as aging baby boomers retire.
Upside pressures on inflation have been counteracted in large part by slowing wage
growth, subdued housing activity, relatively tame credit growth, strong productivity, and
soft unit labor cost increases. In our view, if demand materially exceeded productive
capacity, inflation reacceleration would be much more pervasive.
Also important, the effective tariff rate is expected to average approximately 10%

this year, broadly matching its 2025 average absent another policy shock. The tariff
contribution to inflation is thus expected to continue to fade from its estimated
early-2026 peak of roughly 0.8 percentage point. Further oil-price moderation and
methodological changes affecting financial-service prices and other categories seen
as misrepresenting true inflation will also help improve the picture. Alongside anchored
longer-term inflation expectations, this suggests a gradual further moderation in

inflation, which would improve household purchasing power and support economic

### Page 3

3 of 8 August 31, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
growth. Real consumer spending appears on track for approximately 2.5% to 3% annualized
growth in Q3, following + 0.5% in Q1 and +3.5% in Q2.
Also positive, business investment in equipment and intellectual property accelerated strongly
in the first half. Business investment in manufactured goods is particularly positive for
broadening economic growth given manufacturing activity’s high multiplier effect on jobs and

aggregate demand. Helped by surging AI investment, OBBBA tax incentives, and rebounding
profitability, manufacturing activity is rapidly improving after several weak years, helping
sustain real GDP growth near 2.1% year-over-year, in line with its 25-year average. Although
July “core” capital-goods orders were disappointing, June was revised substantially higher,
raising estimates for Q3 business investment. Strong regional manufacturing surveys also

point to strong momentum, mostly the direct result of surging AI-related investment.
While there is no single accepted estimate for AI investment in the U.S., it is generally
projected at around $600 billion in 2026, $850 billion in 2027, $950 billion in 2028, and over
$1 trillion in 2029. While reaching new highs, its growth rate—and therefore its contribution
to GDP growth—is eventually projected to moderate from +50% to +60% in 2026 and 2027
to +10% by 2029. Also important, its domestic impact is smaller than the headline total

suggests. According to industry estimates, 70% of AI spending is directed toward equipment,
most of it imported. Thus, only around 50% of projected U.S. AI investment is poised to
directly help the domestic economy—though still enough to help broaden growth through
expanding domestic data-center construction, equipment production, power generation, and
supporting infrastructure.
The manufacturing sector’s broader spillovers to suppliers, construction, transportation, and

services make the sector’s recovery an important support for continued expansion. Consistent
with surging AI capital expenditures, rebounding industrial production, and very strong profit
estimates both here and abroad, BofA’s Global Wave 1 indicator has continued to increase—a
typically favorable indicator for cyclical assets’ performance relative to defensives.
With the growth and inflation outlook remaining positive, concerns have shifted to fiscal

strains and uncertainty, as the 2026 deficit appears likely to exceed the Congressional Budget
Office’s February projection by about $200 billion, and publicly-held debt is projected to
rise from approximately 101% of GDP in 2026 to 120% by 2036—a path that could test
investor confidence.
However, since there is no identifiable debt threshold that automatically triggers a crisis,
and comparisons with smaller or externally indebted economies are imperfect, the loss of

confidence in the sustainability of the debt has been short-lived. Indeed, U.S. debt is dollar-
denominated and approximately 70% is domestically held. Deep capital markets and the dollar’s
global reserve currency role sustain structural Treasury demand. Also important, healthy private
sector balance sheets and productivity growth leave scope for gradual fiscal consolidation in
a solidly growing economy without the dire negative effects on the economy observed in past

debt crises overseas. Thus, despite recent concerns, Treasury yields remain within their three-
year range and broadly consistent with moderate growth and anchored inflation expectations,
their recent variations largely tracking energy prices and geopolitical developments.
Also important, the term premium has returned to pre-global-financial-crisis levels following
exceptionally low levels in the post-2008-2009 decade. However, its increase appears to

be just a normalization in a delayed response to previous monetary tightening. Indeed, the
estimated 10-year term premium—or compensation required by investors to hold long-term
debt rather than roll over short-term debt—has historically followed the federal funds rate
with a substantial lag of almost 3 years.
While government debt supply, central-bank balance sheet policy, inflation, and interest-rate
volatility shape the term premium, the lagged fed funds–term premium correlation suggests

that the 2024 to 2025 policy easing is likely to act as a moderating force on the term premium
for the foreseeable future. In our view, the growth and interest rate environment is, therefore,
likely to remain constructive for continued expansion and cyclical assets’ performance,
including materials and infrastructure investments while fiscal uncertainty, soft labor demand,
and an eventually moderating AI investment impulse argue for portfolio diversification.

1 BofA Global Wave quantifies global economic trends, and it is used to forecast stock market performance.

### Page 4

4 of 8 August 31, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Taking Stock of the End of Capital Abundance
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
For much of the past quarter century, the global economy enjoyed a remarkable luxury:
Capital was abundant, cheap and seemingly limitless.
Savings flowed freely across borders. Global interest rates drifted steadily lower and,
for a time, actually turned negative across parts of Europe and Japan. Governments

borrowed with few apparent consequences. Corporations financed growth at historically
attractive rates. Households gained access to inexpensive credit. And investors prospered
as declining discount rates helped push the valuations of stocks, bonds, real estate and
other financial assets higher.
Few countries benefited more from this age of capital abundance than the U.S. As the
world's largest debtor nation and home to the world's deepest capital markets, America

tapped the global savings pool with extraordinary ease. Foreign capital helped finance
persistent federal budget and current account deficits while keeping U.S. borrowing costs
remarkably low. Washington could spend more than it collected, households could consume
more than they earned, and the country as a whole could invest more than it saved.
For decades, the world was happy to finance the difference. That comfortable
arrangement is now being challenged. At precisely the moment when America's financing

needs have rarely been greater, the age of abundant capital is giving way to something
very different: The age of capital competition.
Importantly, the world is not running out of savings. According to the International
Monetary Fund, gross global savings remain around 27% of world GDP , roughly in line
with the average of this century. The supply of savings has not collapsed.
What has changed is the other side of the equation. The world has developed an

enormous appetite for capital. Governments, corporations and households are increasingly
competing for essentially the same pool of savings. And when demand for something
rises faster than its supply, its price tends to rise. For capital, that price is the interest rate.
The past was different. The previous era of abundance was unusual not merely because
the world saved a great deal but because investment demand was comparatively weak.
China, Germany, Japan, oil-producing nations and other surplus economies generated

enormous pools of excess savings. Emerging market central banks accumulated foreign
exchange reserves, much of which found its way into U.S. Treasury securities. Meanwhile,
following the global financial crisis, private investment across much of the developed
world remained subdued.
The upshot: Too much capital was chasing too few attractive and potential investment
opportunities.
Today, that equation is reversing. Global savings remain plentiful, but the list of claimants

on that capital is exploding. We call them the Five Ds: Debt, Defense, Digitalization,
Demographics and Deglobalization.
Start with debt. Governments across the developed world are running large structural
deficits while simultaneously confronting rising interest expenses. The U.S. stands
out. Federal debt held by the public is approaching the size of annual U.S. GDP , and
Washington must finance large new deficits while continually refinancing trillions of

dollars of existing obligations (Exhibits 3 and 4). Treasurys are therefore competing for
capital on a massive scale.
Then comes defense. Russia's invasion of Ukraine, tensions in the Middle East and
strategic competition between the U.S. and China have ended the post-Cold War peace
dividend. America is rebuilding munitions inventories, Europe is rearming, and Asian
Investment Implications
The age of capital competition will likely
favor quality companies with strong

balance sheets, durable cash flows and
capital discipline, in our view. Investors
should consider from the global
capital spending cycle via exposure to
themes such as power and electrical
infrastructure, defense, robotics,
semiconductors and data centers.

### Page 5

5 of 8 August 31, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
nations are increasing military expenditures. Ships, missiles, satellites, drones and
defense-production facilities all require capital.
Third is digitalization. The AI revolution may prove to be among the most capital-
intensive technological transformations in history. Technology companies are spending
extraordinary sums on data centers, advanced semiconductors, networking equipment

and power generation. Utilities need capital to expand and modernize electrical grids.
Semiconductor manufacturers require tens of billions of dollars to build advanced
fabrication plants. All the above is enormously expensive and demands capital.
Fourth is demographics. Aging populations are placing growing pressure on
government budgets through pensions, healthcare and other social programs. Fewer
workers relative to retirees mean greater fiscal burdens across much of the developed

world. In the U.S. alone, mandatory spending outlays on Medicaid, Medicare and Social
Security have jumped from $724 billion in 2000 to $3.2 trillion in FY 2025.
Finally, there is deglobalization. For decades, globalization was fundamentally about
efficiency: Produce goods wherever costs were lowest. Today's world increasingly prizes
resilience and national security, which means the duplication of supply chains, reshored
factories, expanding strategic inventories and subsidized domestic manufacturing

facilities. All these objectives, of course, require capital.
Indeed, the common denominator of debt, defense, digitalization, demographics and
deglobalization is access to capital. And since these factors are structural rather than
cyclical, their demands on global savings are unlikely to fade any time soon.
Evidence of this emerging competition is increasingly visible in financial markets.
Investment-grade companies have already sold nearly $1.5 trillion of bonds this year,

roughly 36% more than during the comparable period last year and on pace to challenge
previous records, according to Bloomberg. With hyperscalers expected to continue
pouring capital into AI infrastructure through the end of the decade, corporate demand
for financing could remain elevated for years.
Meanwhile, the U.S. Treasury must finance enormous federal deficits. The result is an
increasingly crowded marketplace in which governments and corporations are competing

for the same investor dollars. Recent shifts by investment grade bond funds away from
Treasurys and toward corporate debt offer a small but telling glimpse of what this new
environment may look like (Exhibits 5 and 6).
For investors, the implications are significant. First, real interest rates may remain
structurally higher than during much of the previous two decades. Investors waiting for a
return to the ultra-low-rate environment of the 2010s may be waiting a very long time.

Second, fiscal policy matters again. In an era of abundant capital, markets often tolerated
fiscal excess. In a world of greater capital competition, countries that borrow heavily
without generating corresponding economic returns may eventually pay a higher price.
Keep a close eye on Japan.
Third, capital allocation matters more. Companies capable of generating strong returns
on invested capital, maintaining healthy balance sheets and funding expansion internally

should command a premium. When money is no longer virtually free, the difference
between productive investment and financial engineering becomes much more important.
Fourth, the world's enormous capital spending cycle should create winners. Power
generation, electrical infrastructure, defense, industrial automation, semiconductors, data
centers and other capital-intensive industries could enjoy years of elevated investment
and higher-than-expected earnings growth, in our view.

In the end, the age of capital abundance is fading—the era whereby capital basically
chased borrowers is past. Prologue will be defined by borrowers chasing capital. The tide
has turned.

### Page 6

6 of 8 August 31, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What portfolio addition could potentially help mitigate a sharp rise in inflation and/or yields?
How about the start of a new hiking cycle where short-term interest rates rise consistently?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
• If inflation or a weaker U.S. dollar are the reason for a sharp rise in yields, then
increasing exposure to Commodities as a complement to a core portfolio makes sense.

Above-average inflation that is consistent and not excessive mixed with a weaker
U.S. dollar are generally tailwinds for commodities and the companies that mine for
them. Additionally, if global economic growth is running above trend and the U.S. dollar
weakens as it normalizes relative to other currencies, then adding non-U.S Developed
Markets and Emerging Markets (EM) exposure also makes sense.
• If short rates rise sharply due to a new interest rate hiking cycle, a more defensive tilt

in asset exposure makes sense. In other words, in the equity markets more defensive
sectors become more in demand, lower-volatility and higher-quality areas become
more in demand, cyclical shares tend to feel downward pressure, and highly levered
areas without a ton of debt are generally impacted the most. If the short-term rate
rise is minimal and a new hiking cycle is not beginning, then a solid mixture of Value
and Growth Equities, laddered portfolio in bonds, exposure to commodities and

thematic ideas make the most sense overall, in our view.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Personal Consumption Expenditures Price Index is a key economic measure that tracks the average change in prices paid by U.S. consumers for goods and services.
Citi Economic Surprise Index is an economic indicator that measures whether published macroeconomic data is beating or missing market expectations.
Consumer Price Index is a measure of the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative market basket of consumer goods and services.

Investing involves risk, including the possible loss of principal. Past performance is no guarantee of future results.
Bank of America, Merrill, their affiliates and advisors do not provide legal, tax or accounting advice. Clients should consult their legal and/or tax advisors before making any financial decisions.
This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase

or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and
investment advisory services, including the type of advice and assistance provided, the fees charged, and the rights and obligations of the parties. It is important to understand the differences,
particularly when determining which service or services to select. For more information about these services and their differences, speak with your Merrill financial advisor.

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.

oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.
BofA Global Research is research produced by BofA Securities, Inc. (“BofAS”) and/or one or more of its affiliates. BofAS is a registered broker-dealer, Member SIPC and wholly owned subsidiary

of Bank of America Corporation.
All recommendations must be considered in the context of an individual investor’s goals, time horizon, liquidity needs and risk tolerance. Not all recommendations will be in the best interest
of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

Weekly Economics
U.S. Week Ahead
Employment • Friday
We expect nonfarm payroll growth to rebound to 80K in August.
July's drop in employment overstates the degree of labor market
cooling, in our view, with outsized declines in leisure & hospitality
and state & local government education accounting for much
of the downside surprise. We look for partial reversals in both
industries this month. More broadly, alternative indicators suggest
job growth has stabilized, rather than continuing to deteriorate.

Hiring measures from regional Fed surveys have improved in
recent months, small business hiring plans have perked back up,
and job openings have leveled off. Meanwhile, initial jobless claims
remain near multi-decade lows, underscoring that layoffs are still
contained.
We expect the unemployment rate to edge up to 4.2% in August
after dipping to 4.1% in July. The decline in unemployment over the
past year has been driven in part by lower labor force participation,

yet we believe the recent drop overstates the extent to which labor
supply growth is weakening and look for some normalization in
the coming months. Additional flows into the labor force should
place some upward pressure on the unemployment rate. Even so, an
unemployment rate near 4.2% remains low by historical standards
and points to a labor market that is broadly in balance.
Wage growth should continue to suggest a labor market that is
roughly balanced and generating little upward pressure on inflation.

We expect average hourly earnings to rise 0.3% in August following
July's unexpectedly soft reading, leaving the year-over-year pace
in the low-3% range. In general, the jobs report should reinforce
the view that labor demand is tepid, but remains sufficient to keep
layoffs low and support modest job growth.

Advanced Economies
Eurozone CPI • Tuesday
We expect Eurozone inflation to accelerate in August, with headline
CPI rising to 3.4% year over year (0.6% month over month) and
core CPI edging up to 2.6% year over year. Strong August inflation
prints from France and Spain point to a firmer Eurozone reading
next week, reinforcing the case for a September European Central

Bank rate hike.
July inflation had already reaccelerated, with headline CPI rising to
2.9% year over year, core inflation to 2.5% and services inflation
to 3.3% year over year after easing in June. At the same time, ECB
consumer inflation expectations continued to moderate, with 1-
year-ahead expectations at 2.9% and 3-year-ahead expectations
at 2.7% in July. While still above the ECB's 2% target, expectations
have moved lower from the highs seen following the start of the

conflict.
We continue to expect the ECB to raise the Deposit Rate by 25
bps to 2.50% in September. Risks remain skewed toward additional
tightening if inflation continues to broaden and evidence of second-
round effects becomes more apparent. For now, however, we view
2.50% as the terminal rate through 2027.

Economics Week Ahead Economics

Bank of Canada Monetary Policy Meeting • Wednesday
We expect the Bank of Canada (BoC) to leave the policy rate
unchanged at 2.25% next week. Since policymakers opted to remain
on hold at the previous meeting, incoming data have provided little
reason to alter that stance. Core inflation measures have continued
to track close to 2%, suggesting underlying price pressures remain
well contained despite ongoing uncertainty surrounding the conflict

in Iran and trade policy.
The labor market has also held up better than feared. Employment
growth has remained positive, and the unemployment rate has
stabilized in recent months, supporting the view that economic
activity is slowing, but not deteriorating materially. While pockets
of weakness remain evident across interest rate-sensitive sectors,
economic conditions no longer appear soft enough to warrant
additional near-term easing. The economy expanded at a 3.3%

annualized clip in the second quarter, marking the strongest
quarterly rate of growth in nearly two years.
The data have largely validated the BoC's decision to pause. Inflation
is near target, hiring is holding up, and growth has remained
surprisingly resilient. That leaves policymakers comfortably on hold,
but the path forward is far less clear. A reacceleration in inflation
could reopen the door to rate hikes, while escalating U.S. trade
tensions raise the risk of a sharper slowdown that would warrant

renewed easing. For now, those risks appear broadly balanced,
giving the BoC little reason to move in either direction.

Australia GDP • Wednesday
We expect Australia’s Q2 GDP growth to remain resilient at 1.9%

year over year (0.4% quarter over quarter) despite headwinds from
the war in the Middle East and higher interest rate. Household
consumption likely provided a solid contribution to growth, while
business investment was more mixed. Private capex softened in
Q2, though weakness was concentrated in information media
and telecommunications equipment. Excluding that category,
machinery and equipment investment remained firm, while total
capital expenditure was still up 10.7% year over year. Net exports

likely weighed on growth again as import growth continued to
outpace exports.
Overall, the economy appears to have held up well despite a
challenging backdrop. With July CPI broadly in line with our
expectations, but firmer than markets anticipated, a stronger-than-
expected GDP print would reinforce our call for one final Reserve
Bank of Australia rate hike, taking the Cash Rate to a terminal
4.60% by year-end.
